24 & Sep 14--15 & \textbf{sketch target}: supervised thought = span inside the window, offset 8--48; unbiased $\mathrm{ob}_0$ logged; from run 21 @7700 & \textbf{Reader fixed, writer not.} Oracle gain $-1.4 \to +7.4$; own sampled thought $-2.5 \to -2.2$; $\mathrm{ob}_0 = -0.2$; imitation CE $3.7$; held-out $+0.086$. \\
\addlinespace
25 & Sep 17--18 & \textbf{reader calibration}: reader loss only on one random own thought; REINFORCE on that rollout; RL $0.5$; greedy thought scored; from run 24 @3800 & \textbf{Calibration bought, content not.} Held-out think-mode greedy gain $-2.45 \to -1.17$ by step $900$ (the programme's largest held-out move), then flat ($-1.26$ at $1{,}500$); oracle gain $+6.3 \to +3.9$; $\mathrm{ob}_0 = -0.149 \pm 0.015$, never positive; held-out CE $+0.082$. Stopped at ${\sim}1{,}530$ for futility. \\
\addlinespace
26 & Sep 18 & \textbf{pause-token control}: every thought slot is content-free filler; no decode, no rollouts, no writer loss; reader trained on the same window rows; same initialisation as run 25 & \textbf{Filler wins; the gain was compute.} Training gain of the filler slot $-0.49 \to -0.03$ over $1{,}500$ steps, against run 25's written thought at $-2.12 \to -1.53$ over the same steps---${\sim}1.5$ nats better at every block. On run 25's \emph{own} weights the filler beats its written thought held-out. Held-out CE $+0.025$ at $1{,}400$, against run 25's $+0.082$ at $1{,}500$: the filler is cheaper in next-token prediction too. \\
\addlinespace
27 & Sep 18 & \textbf{greedy-baseline RL}: a sample is reinforced only when it beats the thought the model would actually emit (\texttt{-{}-baseline greedy}); one flag changed from run 25; from run 25 @1500 & \textbf{Stopped at step $826$ by its own pre-registered futility rule.} $\mathrm{ob}_0(500\text{--}799) = -0.097 \pm 0.017$ against a required $+0.05$; $g_{r0}$ gained $+0.278$ against a required $+0.30$. Run 25's signature reproduced exactly: of the $+0.278$, $+0.224$ is shared with the thought's own shuffle, so it is register drift, and $\mathrm{ob}_0$ was never positive. The REINFORCE baseline is not the bottleneck. Held-out on its own step-$800$ weights, its written thought scores $-0.857 \pm 0.112$ against content-free filler's $-0.503 \pm 0.096$ at matched length: it loses to filler by $-0.355 \pm 0.148$, the same sign and a comparable size to run 25's $-0.433 \pm 0.134$. \\
\addlinespace
28 & Sep 18 & \textbf{near-horizon forecast}: supervised thought = the span starting $0$--$4$ tokens after the anchor (was $8$--$48$), thoughts allowed to be short and priced at $0.15$ nats/token; from run 25 @1500 & \textbf{Stopped at step ${\sim}860$ by its own pre-registered single-clause rule.} $\mathrm{ob}_0(500\text{--}799) = -0.127 \pm 0.016$ against a required $+0.05$, flat since step zero; of $g_{r0}$'s $+0.369$ gain, $+0.311$ is shared with the thought's own shuffle. But the target was the right diagnosis: free-running token agreement at $m{=}2$ went $0.042 \to 0.113$, the first time a run's thought was distinguishable from the unigram base rate---and own $-$ filler got \emph{worse} at every length, because partial correctness is the worst place to be. Section~\ref{sec:run28}. \\
\addlinespace
P3 & Sep 18 & \emph{abstention ladder} (Table~\ref{tab:abst}): is an interleaved pause readable, and does the model know where it is right? & Both preconditions pass. Pause-filled break-even is $2/12$ against context-noise's $4/12$; the model's top-$2$ confident positions are right $81.3\%$ of the time against $44.2\%$ unselected. Yet every confidence-selected forecast still scores below the filler floor, short of break-even precision by $0.03$--$0.16$ at every width. \\
\addlinespace
P4 & Sep 18 & \emph{teacher-forced-in-slot} (Table~\ref{tab:tfslot}): is the $11\%$-vs-$44\%$ gap exposure bias, or a slot that no longer forecasts? & \textbf{Exposure bias, decisively.} Given a true prefix the slot reaches $0.42$ by position five against the base model's $0.44$, and averages $0.347$ over positions $1$--$11$ where the free-running thought scores $0.019$. Break-even is $0.167$, so the capability is present and above the bar; only the decoding regime destroys it. Section~\ref{sec:tfslot}. \\
P5 & Sep 18 & The same probe on run 29 @500, whose writer is supervised on the immediate continuation: is the position-$0$ collapse real, and does a better target move the free-running writer? & \textbf{The opener was mistargeted, not broken, and the writer moved furthest yet.} Position $0$ goes $0.047 \to 0.297$ once it is actually supervised on $x_{n+1}$, so P4's ``separately broken opener'' is retracted. Free-running agreement over positions $1$--$11$ rises $3.4\times$ ($0.0193 \to 0.0657$) with \textsc{ntp} unchanged, while the teacher-forced ceiling does not move ($0.347 \to 0.366$): fidelity was bought, not capability. Still about half a bar short ($1.02$ correct tokens of the $\approx 2$ needed). Table~\ref{tab:tfslot29}. \\
P6 & Sep 19 & \emph{non-autoregressive slot} (\texttt{probe\_tfslot.py -{}-nar}) on run 29 @600 and run 30 @600: if error propagation is the whole loss, does a slot with no prefix to propagate recover the teacher-forced ceiling? & \textbf{No---it is worse than free-running.} Over positions $1$--$11$, $\textsc{nar}$ $0.020$ against $\textsc{own}$ $0.071$ and $\textsc{tfslot}$ $0.330$ (run 29); $0.018$ against $0.069$ and $0.274$ (run 30). The alignment identity $\textsc{nar}@1 = \textsc{own}@1 = 0.305$ holds exactly. Read as a lower bound, since the parallel regime is never trained: no free lunch, not a refutation of parallel decoding. Section~\ref{sec:run30}. \\
\addlinespace
29 & Sep 18 & \textbf{speculative copy}: one value changed from run 28, \texttt{-{}-oracle-mode fixed -{}-oracle-off 0}, removing the five-way offset lottery that capped run 28's agreement at $0.088$ by construction; from run 25 @1500 & \textbf{Stopped at step ${\approx}780$} on its registered $\mathrm{ob}_0(500\text{--}799) \ge +0.05$ gate. But its free-running writer was the best measured: $\textsc{own}(1\text{--}11)$ $0.0605 \to 0.0657 \to 0.0751$ at steps $300/500/600$, still rising when it was killed---by a gate the abstention ladder has since shown cannot fire below ${\approx}2$ correct tokens of $12$. Table~\ref{tab:tfslot29}. \\
\addlinespace
30 & Sep 18--19 & \textbf{scheduled sampling / DAgger} (\texttt{-{}-dagger-p 0.8} ramped over steps $100$--$900$) plus \texttt{-{}-slot-pos aligned}; from run 25 @1500, so matched to runs 27--29 & \textbf{Stopped at step ${\approx}690$; the bar failed and the ceiling fell.} $\textsc{own}(1\text{--}11) = 0.0689$ against a registered $0.15$ and a matched control of $0.0751$---below its own control---while $\textsc{tfslot}(1\text{--}11)$ collapsed $0.385 \to 0.274$ in $300$ steps. Splicing own inputs onto true targets degrades the teacher-forced pathway it was meant to unlock. The aligned register survives ($+0.0096$). Section~\ref{sec:run30}, Table~\ref{tab:run30}. \\
\addlinespace
31 & Sep 19 & \textbf{the same design, run long}: run 30 with \texttt{-{}-dagger-p 0.0} and nothing else changed, i.e.\ run 29 plus the aligned register, to $2{,}000$ steps; from run 25 @1500 & \textbf{Stopped at step ${\approx}860$ on gate one.} $\textsc{own}(1\text{--}11) = 0.0722$ at step $600$ against a registered $0.0751$---inside one standard error of the control, so the decisive reading is the other one: at matched register and matched $n=616$ the run's \emph{own} trajectory is flat, $0.0716$ @300 $\to 0.0722$ @600. Run 29's slope was a climb to the same level, not a rate. Fourth independent measurement of the free-running floor. No derail axis fired (held-out mixture $+0.018$ of a $+0.25$ allowance). Section~\ref{sec:run31}. \\

\addlinespace
32 & Sep 19 & \textbf{abstention / pmix supervision}: the imitation target keeps the true token only where it is one of the $300$ most frequent unigrams and holds content-free filler elsewhere (\texttt{-{}-abstain-mode freq}); two flags from run 31, and initialised from run 31 @800 to keep its opener & \textbf{Stopped at $1130$.} The first design that attacks the \emph{cost} of wrong tokens rather than the writer's accuracy, which four measurements say does not move. Gates on $\textsc{own}(1\text{--}11)$: $\ge 0.0722$ at $600$ (do no harm), $\ge 0.105$ at $1000$, $\ge 0.154$ at $1500$ = two correctly-placed tokens of twelve = break-even against filler. Step $300$ was a diagnostic, not a gate, and it read $\textsc{own}(1\text{--}11) = 0.0323$ at a filler rate of $83\%$. \textbf{The step-$600$ gate FAILED}: $0.0397 \pm 0.0024$ against $0.0722$, a factor of $1.8$ below the bar, at a filler rate now $88\%$ and a stop rate of $0.001$---while precision on the one position in eight where it speaks rose to $0.335$, near the teacher-forced ceiling of $0.347$. The bar itself was too generous: the run's own initialisation scores $0.0775$ on the same instrument. \textbf{STOPPED at $1130$} on the hard stop registered at $1000$. The value instrument, registered before step $600$ existed and amended before its data arrived, split cleanly: improvement over its own initialisation $+0.375 \pm 0.078$ ($m{=}4$) and $+0.702 \pm 0.094$ ($m{=}8$), but own-minus-filler itself $-0.110 \pm 0.030$ and $-0.132 \pm 0.033$, still below the blank slot. \textbf{It stopped harming; it did not start helping.} Half the gain is the registered failure mode---$230$ of $465$ anchors emit filler and nothing else, against $0$ for its initialisation---and half is real: where it speaks, harm fell from $-0.711$ to $-0.261$ at $m{=}8$. The step-$1000$ agreement gate of $0.105$ is withdrawn as unreachable rather than quietly missed. Fifth checkpoint below its own filler floor, and the first to come close. At step $1000$ the quantity is flat---own-minus-filler $-0.130 \pm 0.033$ ($m{=}4$), a paired change of $-0.020 \pm 0.033$ over $400$ steps---so it asymptoted below zero rather than toward it. Its successor was falsified without being run: the registered gate for confidence-selected abstention is $+0.021 \pm 0.066$ at $m{=}7$. Section~\ref{sec:run32}. \\
\addlinespace
33 & Sep 19 & \textbf{make the block free}: run 32 with one flag added, \texttt{-{}-pause-frac 0.5}---half the anchors carry content-free filler, leave both writer objectives and join the reader's; from run 32 @1000 & \textbf{Launched 18:12.} The first run to attack the larger term: on run 32 @1000 the block costs $-0.926 \pm 0.095$ and what the thought says costs a further $-0.200 \pm 0.042$ on the same instrument (the $-0.130 \pm 0.033$ quoted earlier is the draft probe at a four-token prefix), and run 26 shows the first can reach $-0.083 \pm 0.021$. Launched on a fork resolved in its favour the same day: run 26's filler-trained reader values a perfect hint at $+12.62 \pm 0.42$ against its sibling's $+10.72$, so a cheap block is not a deaf one. Gates: paired improvement $\ge 0.30$ nats over $-0.926$ at step $400$ and $\ge 0.55$ at $800$ (mechanism), own-minus-filler no worse than the initialisation's at $800$ (overrides both), own-minus-filler $>0$ at $1500$. Registered before the data: if the block's price falls all the way and the content term holds, the absolute measurement improves by $0.7$ nats and \emph{the bar is still failed}---the block's price is the term that cancels in it. \textbf{Step-400 gate passed}: the block's price is $-0.551 \pm 0.082$, a paired $+0.376 \pm 0.053$ over the initialisation, $45\%$ of the available headroom. \textbf{STOPPED at $899$: the step-$800$ gate FAILED and the mechanism reversed}---$-0.664 \pm 0.081$, a paired $+0.262 \pm 0.048$ against a bar of $+0.55$, six standard errors below it and \emph{down} from $+0.376$ at step $400$. The value gate fails too ($-1.147 \pm 0.284$ against the initialisation's $-0.200 \pm 0.042$) but must not be read as content: on this lineage the greedy thought \emph{is} the control string---space tokens then the closing marker---so the comparison measures filler length. Binned by length, run 32 @1000 reads $+0.023$ at eight tokens and $-0.131 \pm 0.089$ at ten (matched, not significant), which retires its $-0.200$ as two-thirds a length artefact; run 33 lost its stop action ($10.45 \to 13.9$ tokens) and pays $-0.49$ and $-1.20$ for fourteen and fifteen tokens of pure whitespace. The block's price is steeply superlinear in length, and value on an abstaining lineage must be read from the length-matched draft probe. Section~\ref{sec:run33}. \\
\addlinespace
34 & Sep 20 & \textbf{make the writer speak}: run 32 with one flag added, \texttt{-{}-abstain-loss-w 0.0}---the abstention blanks keep their place in the block the model reads but carry no weight in the imitation loss, so silence stops being a supervised action; from run 31 @800, which is run 32's own initialisation & \textbf{Launched 00:20.} Run 32's writer is mute: its filler rate over positions one to eleven is $0.83$/$0.88$/$0.74$ at steps $300$/$600$/$1000$ against $0.025$ for its own initialisation, so ``it stopped harming'' reads more honestly as ``it stopped speaking''. What it did buy survives---precision where it speaks $0.187 \to 0.335$, against a teacher-forced ceiling of $0.347$. Run 34 occupies the untested cell: a writer that speaks \emph{and} is trained only on reachable targets, asking whether a spoken token is worth more to the reader than a blank. Gates, registered before any data: filler rate $\le 0.50$ at step $300$ (mechanism); at step $600$ both that bound \emph{and} own-minus-filler at four- and eight-token prefixes no worse than run 32's $-0.110$/$-0.132$ by more than two standard errors---the first clause is present because total silence maximises the second at exactly zero; own-minus-filler $>0$ at $1200$. Registered reading: landing between $-0.110$ and $-0.485$ means precision did not survive volume and the negative result is complete. \textbf{STOPPED at $886$: the mechanism clause passed and the value clause failed by six and nine standard errors.} The filler rate falls to $0.046$/$0.043$ at steps $300$/$600$ ($n{=}616$), against run 32's $0.827$/$0.881$ at the same steps---the flag un-mutes the writer completely---but own-minus-filler is $-0.520 \pm 0.067$ and $-0.892 \pm 0.083$ at four- and eight-token prefixes, against bars of $-0.110$/$-0.132$ and \emph{below} the fully speaking control that is its own initialisation ($-0.485$/$-0.834$). Against speaking rate the three checkpoints lie on one line---$0.119 \to -0.110$, $0.957 \to -0.520$, $0.975 \to -0.485$---so value tracks how much is said and not how well: run 32's precision of $0.335$ where it spoke was a selection effect, and run 34 is the more accurate writer by token agreement ($0.149$ against $0.089$ at a four-token prefix) while being worth less. The registered reading applies and the negative result is complete. The think-mode probe agrees on the final checkpoint (step $800$, $n{=}465$ paired): own-minus-filler $-0.81 \pm 0.08$, and $-0.69 \pm 0.17$ on the anchors where the thought is exactly the filler's nine tokens long---on this lineage the greedy thought is words rather than filler, so the contrast is content and not length. \textbf{Benchmarks (step $800$, token-matched vanilla \texttt{step\_0026600}):} the pre-registered five-task pool is $+0.12$ points ($p = 0.91$), so the discrimination gain of run 21 is gone; clean-mode macro $-2.5$ with LAMBADA $-13.4$, but the paired contrast from its own initialisation (run 31 @ $800$) is macro $-0.4$ and LAMBADA $+0.4$ ($p = 0.47$ pooled), so the loss is inherited and speaking is downstream-neutral; think mode at $L{=}16$ in the aligned register is $-1.4$ macro against the same weights ($p = 4\times10^{-4}$ over $4{,}000$ examples), and its greedy thoughts are $258$ distinct strings over $4{,}000$ examples. Sections~\ref{sec:run34} and~\ref{sec:bench}. \\
\addlinespace
35 & Sep 22 & \textbf{the bridge}: the last $32$ context tokens are hidden from the window rows but stay visible to the thought block (\texttt{-{}-bridge 32}), so the writer's job becomes \emph{carrying} (teacher-forced in-slot accuracy $0.30$--$0.41$) instead of forecasting (free-running $0.06$); from run 34 @800 & \textbf{Stopped at step ${\approx}250$: $g_{r0} = -0.32$ against a registered $+0.5$ at step $300$, and falling.} The gate is not the result. Priced against \emph{silence under the same mask} ($n{=}154$ held-out anchors), the reader pays $+9.85 \pm 0.59$ for the true future tokens and $-0.27 \pm 0.18$ for the exact hidden text it is missing---its $16$-token tail, $+0.57 \pm 0.19$, is the only positive anywhere in the table, while the model's own thought reads $+0.10 \pm 0.17$ and filler $-0.03 \pm 0.11$. Hiding the span costs ${\approx}8$ nats and carrying it back recovers none of them. \textbf{The slot's only currency is text shaped like the continuation}, so re-aiming the writer at an easier target cannot work however well it hits it. Same probe, same tokens, one closing marker apart: $-19.63$ unterminated against $-0.27$ terminated---termination is worth $19$ nats and is not cosmetic. Section~\ref{sec:run35}. \\
\addlinespace
36 & Sep 22 & \textbf{bridge anneal} (\texttt{-{}-bridge} $32 \to 0$ over training), written as run 35's successor & \textbf{Never launched.} Dead on arrival on run 35's own probe: annealing the width changes how much the reader is missing, and the measurement says it does not pay for what it is missing at any width. Configuration kept in the tree, unrun, as part of the record. \\
\addlinespace
37 & Sep 22--23 & \textbf{a learned code, pure reinforcement}: the thought alphabet is $64$ reserved ids with no pretrained meaning (\texttt{-{}-code-size 64}), initialised to a neutral token's embedding so a block costs ${\approx}0$ instead of $-14$ nats; reward only; from run 25 @1500 & \textbf{Stopped at step ${\approx}420$ on the registered collapse-to-a-constant axis.} Rollout spread $0.03$ against a bar of $0.6$; order-borne value $-0.001 \pm 0.001$, flat from step zero; unselected gain, its shuffle, greedy and best-of-eight agree to three decimals at every block. Mechanism measured on the checkpoints, not inferred: mean pairwise distance between the $64$ code embeddings $0.01532 \to 0.01609$ over $400$ steps, against $1.016$ for ordinary token pairs. \textbf{A neutral initialisation is a \emph{symmetric} one, and symmetry is a fixed point of reinforcement}---equal embeddings, equal rollout scores, zero advantage, no differentiating gradient. Its other gate passed while the run was dead ($g_{r0}$ $+0.763$, all of it the block getting cheaper), which is the $g_{r0}$ decomposition catching a gate we wrote ourselves. Section~\ref{sec:code}. \\
\addlinespace
38 & Sep 23 & \textbf{a supervised symmetry-breaker}: run 37 plus \texttt{-{}-code-teacher 1}, a $64$-way hash of the most frequent content token in each half of the upcoming window, supervising a quarter of the anchors & \textbf{Stopped at step ${\approx}410$; both pre-registered gates failed.} Code rows $0.01543$ @200 and $0.01617$ @400 against $\ge 0.05$ and rising; order-borne value $-0.002$ and spread $0.031$ against $+0.05$ and $0.30$. The imitation cross-entropy fell $11.11 \to 2.79$ in a hundred steps and looked like convergence; $\tfrac{2}{3}\ln 64 = 2.7726$ is \emph{guessing uniformly}, and it sat there to three decimals for three hundred more steps, having learned the format and nothing else (information recovered per symbol $-0.005$ to $+0.022$ nats of $4.159$, non-monotone). The target was checked on the corpus and is not degenerate ($64/64$ symbols, entropy $3.922$, top share $0.102$). \textbf{A cross-entropy target breaks a symmetry only if it is predictable from the model's context}; a hash of text the writer has not read is not, so its Bayes solution is the uniform distribution---the symmetric point itself. Section~\ref{sec:code}. \\
\addlinespace
39 & Sep 23 & \textbf{the channel control}, bounded at $300$ steps: \texttt{-{}-code-teacher 2}, the hash of a \emph{single in-context token} the model can already see (the token $32$ back, then the anchor), $90\%$ supervised anchors and reinforcement weight zero---a capacity measurement made maximally favourable to the channel; from run 37/38's initialisation & \textbf{Ran to $300$ and both registered gates failed.} It exists because an uninformative-target failure and a dead-channel failure are indistinguishable in the imitation cross-entropy; a pure lookup separates them. Baselines computed on the corpus before the first step (entropies $4.012$/$4.009$ of $4.159$; marginal floor $2.6737$, uniform $2.7726$). The gate---CE below $2.00$ over steps $150$--$199$, unreachable by learning the marginal---read $2.724$, and the code rows went $0.01532 \to 0.01565$ against $0.05$ (untied output rows $0.03605 \to 0.03760$ against $1.194$). \textbf{The shape is the result}: by the last block the channel had recovered $0.141$ of the $0.148$ nats per symbol available from a context-\emph{free} marginal and none of the $4.011$ nats sitting in its own context, asymptoting at the marginal floor from above and stopping $0.007$ short of it. Spread $0.032$, as in runs 37 and 38. This re-attributes both: the corrected rule keeps ``initialise fresh ids with symmetry-breaking noise'' and loses ``or supply an outside supervised target''. The coded route is nonetheless closed by run 35's probe and not by these three runs---a private code is not forecast-shaped text, so a perfect alphabet would still have earned the filler floor. Section~\ref{sec:code}. \\
\addlinespace
40--69 & Sep 24--Oct 1 & synthetic pointer-chasing task (40--59) and the slide from compression to forecast (60--69) & Recorded in Sections~\ref{sec:synthetic} and~\ref{sec:slide}. \\
\addlinespace
70 & Oct 1--2 & \textbf{frozen reader}: every note and baseline scored by a frozen copy of the initialisation; writer trained on the plain reward only; slide $o=-15 \to +1$ & \textbf{Stopped at its first gate}: own-minus-filler $+4.47 \pm 0.59$ at $o=-15$ @400 against a $+9.00$ bar (untrained $+6.93$); @500 read $+6.91$, level with the initialisation. \\
\addlinespace
71 & Oct 2 & run 70 plus copy-teaching (imitation of the true span on a quarter of the anchors) & Compression rungs far above the initialisation ($+13.23$ at $o=-15$, $+12.55$ at $o=-9$); forecast rung $-4.07 \pm 0.75$ against filler (untrained $-7.98$), own-minus-shuffle $+2.79$ (untrained $+0.53$). Section~\ref{sec:frozen}. \\
\addlinespace
72, 74, 77, 78 & Oct 2 & registered successors (reward-only resume of 70; priced abstention; hindsight without price; hindsight with a $64$-sample student) & \textbf{Never launched}: superseded by the gradient-synchronisation fix (72, 74) or by the constant-note finding, under which their own-versus-silence reward collapses (77, 78). \\
\addlinespace
73 & Oct 2 & run 71 resumed and held at $o=+1$ (more forecast compute) & $-3.08 \pm 0.77$ @3000, a paired $+0.99 \pm 0.27$ over run 71; content gain over the initialisation at every offset read. Stopped when the synchronisation error was found. \\
\addlinespace
75 & Oct 2 & run 71 with synchronised gradients & Smoke only: proved the fix (one replica checksum across $64$ ranks against $26$ without it); preempted for the hindsight runs. \\
\addlinespace
76 & Oct 2 & \textbf{hindsight to foresight} (teacher sees the next $64$ tokens, $G=64$, priced; student imitates and runs its own $16$-sample update), $64$ ranks & \textbf{Never trained}: three smokes died on device memory, device memory again, and host memory ($30.7$\,GB per rank). \\
\addlinespace
76R & Oct 3 & run 76 at $32$ ranks $\times$ two accumulation steps & First launch void (top-$k$ index error: every note noise). Relaunched with a Gumbel sampler: \textbf{collapsed by step 50} to rows of digits in teacher and student alike; best-of-$64$ unchanged ($+5.29 \to +5.20$). Section~\ref{sec:frozen}. \\
\addlinespace
79 & Oct 2--3 & run 73 continued with synchronised gradients & Progress gate fired ($z = 5.7$) and was overruled: $54\%$ of sampled notes had become one repeated digit by step $4{,}188$. Stopped. \\
\addlinespace
80 & Oct 3 & run 73's line with the contrastive reward (note versus the neighbour's note) & Paused at step ${\approx}3{,}009$ to give the device to run 81. \\
\addlinespace
81 & Oct 3 & hindsight to foresight with the contrastive reward & \textbf{Stopped at step $44$} by its armed collapse probe: the greedy note's repetition share passed $0.30$, the bar registered before launch. The contrastive reward prices a constant note at exactly zero and still did not prevent the collapse---what collapsed is the \emph{teacher}, whose $G=64$ hindsight samples degenerate together. \\
\addlinespace
82 & Oct 3 & run 81 plus a specificity filter on the imitation target: the student copies only teacher notes that are clearly specific and non-repetitive & \textbf{Stopped at step $13$}, not on a verdict: the owner halted the device to discuss the programme. The hindsight-to-foresight line was not resumed; what replaced it is the entropy-triggered family below, which asks the model to think only where its own next-token distribution says it is uncertain. \\
\addlinespace
83 & Oct 3 & \textbf{entropy-triggered thinking}, the first of the family: a thought is written only at anchors where the model's own next-token entropy exceeds $\tau = 4$ ($16$ per sequence, $L = 64$), kept if it beats silence by $1$ nat, and imitated where kept; trained on the vanilla run's own unseen tokens, so the equal-data control is the vanilla ladder itself & \textbf{Stopped at step ${\approx}195$, deranged.} By step $120$--$180$ every thought is eight tokens of the form \texttt{' is'} followed by a seven-digit number (\texttt{' is2422997'}, \texttt{' is9919139'}); full-deployment drop $-4.2$ nats, imitation cross-entropy $1.1$, and a control thought passed the keep test $3.7\%$ of the time against $7.6\%$ for the model's own---i.e.\ most of what was kept was kept by chance, and imitating it collapsed the writer. \\
\addlinespace
84 & Oct 3 & run 83 with the keep margin doubled ($1 \to 2$ nats): the obvious reading of run 83 is that the bar was too low & \textbf{Stopped at step ${\approx}218$: the same attractor, one margin higher.} Thoughts are bare seven-digit numbers (digit share $0.67$), generated length $64 \to 12$, deployment drop $-4.6$. The margin did change one thing, and it is the thing that mattered: the kept rate is now $1.5\%$ against $0.4\%$ for the control, so these passes are \emph{not} chance---a digit string genuinely primes the reader at numeric positions and clears any loss margin \emph{by format alone}. Raising a loss bar cannot distinguish content from format, because format is what a loss bar measures. \\
\addlinespace
85 & Oct 3 & run 84's lesson applied: a thought is kept only if it beats silence \emph{and} its own shuffle, each by $1$ nat, at the same prefix---a content test rather than a louder loss test; margin back to $1$ & \textbf{Stopped at step ${\approx}228$, deranged differently.} The content test did remove the digit attractor (digit share $0.00$), which is the first direct evidence that the shuffle control works as a reward term and not only as a diagnostic. The writer collapsed anyway, to a template: by step $160$--$220$ every thought reads \texttt{' is a <noun> \ldots'} (opener share $0.46$), generated length $64 \to 7$, imitation cross-entropy $1.9 \to 0.19$, and the kept rate falls $2.6\% \to 0.3\%$---nothing passes any more. \textbf{The cause is the imitation term, not the keep rule}: copying the model's own few surviving thoughts at full weight collapses its thought distribution within ${\approx}150$ steps whichever rule selects them. That is what run 86 tests, at one tenth the imitation weight. \\
\addlinespace
86 & Oct 3 & run 85 at one tenth the imitation weight ($1.0 \to 0.1$): the writer still learns from its own kept thoughts, slowly & \textbf{Stopped at step $900$} on a rung-1 conjunction registered before the data, met on all three clauses at $10$--$15$ standard errors: the model's thoughts are now \emph{worse than their own shuffles} (\texttt{own\_shuf} $-1.860 \pm 0.186$, against $+0.121 \pm 0.039$ at the initialisation), the full-deployment drop reads $-4.776 \pm 0.252$ (initialisation $-0.876$, a $15$-s.e.\ worsening; thoughts help $9\%$ of the time against $27\%$), and a symmetric control thought cleared the keep test $2.6\%$ of the time against $1.6\%$ for the model's own---most of what survives the content test still survives it by chance. Clean held-out mix $2.7928$ against the vanilla rung's $2.7736$. Diluting imitation delayed the collapse and changed no verdict; the successor registered at the stop was \texttt{--imit-w 0}. \\
\addlinespace
87 & Oct 3--4 & run 86 with imitation removed entirely (\texttt{--imit-w} $0.1 \to 0$), i.e.\ reader-only: the writer is never taught to copy its kept thoughts & \textbf{Stopped at step $2{,}421$} by the armed base-damage monitor (clean held-out $2.8057$ against the vanilla rung's $2.7722$, past the $+0.03$ bar registered before launch). It separated two failures the family had been reading as one. \textbf{Imitation is what collapses the writer}: matched step for step against run 86---same base, same block offset, same data seed, only the weight differs---the generated length stays pinned at $64.0$ for $598$ steps, against $64 \to 6$ by step $450$ at weight $0.1$, and the kept rate \emph{rises} $2.9\% \to 5.2\%$ where run 86's fell, because the writer is no longer being dragged onto the filter's own mode. \textbf{But deployment damage is a separate failure}: the drop worsens monotonically with imitation off anyway ($-0.78 \to -2.13$), and run 86 was already at $-1.70$ over steps $200$--$300$, \emph{before} its length had moved at all. Paying nats to deploy a thought therefore precedes and survives the length collapse, and it is the thing we care about. One confound recorded rather than discovered later: \texttt{--imit-w 0} is not a frozen writer---the imitation cross-entropy, still measured as a free diagnostic, rose $1.97 \to 2.76$, because the reader's loss flows through the shared trunk. \\
\addlinespace
88 & Oct 4 & the reader trained on \emph{all} thoughts rather than only the kept ones, so that training and deployment match; no imitation & \textbf{Stopped at step $965$} by the owner once the question was answered: training the reader on everything makes the reader \textbf{deaf}. The deployment drop goes $-0.51 \to -0.03$, the kept rate $2.3\% \to 0.0\%$, and the rung-1 probe reads $-0.037 \pm 0.024$ with thoughts helping $49\%$ of the time---they neither help nor hurt. The load-bearing by-product is about the baseline, not the thinking: its clean held-out at rung 1 is $2.7927$ against vanilla's $2.7736$, the \emph{same} $+0.019$ as runs 86 and 87, although its thinking losses were ${\approx}0$ from step ${\approx}300$ onwards. \textbf{The base damage this family had been charging to thinking is the $65$k-token batch at the vanilla learning rate}, not the thinking losses. A separate batch-size control, queued as run 88, was cancelled as unnecessary once this run had settled the question. \\
\addlinespace
89 & Oct 4 & \textbf{GRPO} on thoughts ($8$ spots per sequence, $G=8$, $L=32$, top and bottom two of each group) with reader cross-entropy on the good samples and KL-to-clean on the bad; first run back on the vanilla batch & \textbf{Stopped at step $190$} by the owner at rung 1: the reader went deaf again (group effect $-0.64 \to -0.05$, fool KL $0.77 \to 0.10$) and the writer collapsed onto a single opener (opener share $0.13 \to 0.81$) \emph{despite} the explicit negatives on the bottom two of each group. It does confirm the previous run's attribution: back on the vanilla batch the base gap is zero ($2.7732$ against vanilla's $2.7736$). Successor: the same design with a frozen initialisation reader and KL-to-init on the bad samples. \\
\addlinespace
90 & Oct 4 & run 89 with the reader \textbf{frozen at the initialisation} and a KL-to-init term on the bad samples, so the referee cannot co-adapt; GRPO at $\textrm{rl-}w = 0.1$ & \textbf{Stopped at step $193$, no movement of any kind.} Own-minus-shuffle $+0.083 \to +0.108$, deployment drop $-0.77 \to -0.73$, generated length $32.0$ flat, KL to the reference $0.029$ nats: the writer's thought distribution is still the initialisation's. (We originally listed ``opener share $0.131$, flat'' in that evidence and have removed it: that statistic is the share of the most common three-token prefix among the thoughts \emph{inside one micro-batch}, taken over one sample of the $G$ drawn per anchor, and a modal share over $m$ items cannot fall below $1/m$. With ${\approx}8$ watched thoughts its floor is $0.126$, run 90 never left that floor by more than $0.014$ on any of $207$ logged steps, and so the number cannot distinguish ``unchanged from the initialisation'' from ``changed but still diverse''. It is the length and the KL that carry the claim. The same statistic is informative when it \emph{moves}: run 89's $0.13 \to 0.81$ is $7.9\times$ its floor, and run 85's $0.46$ likewise.) The cause is arithmetic, not conceptual---at $\textrm{rl-}w = 0.1$ the REINFORCE term is about $1.3\%$ of the clean next-token gradient. The row is kept because of its baseline: on the vanilla batch the base gap is \emph{zero} ($2.7732$ against vanilla's $2.7736$), the first confirmation of run 88's attribution. Successor: the same design at $\textrm{rl-}w = 1.0$. \\
\addlinespace
91 & Oct 4--5 & run 90 at standard GRPO proportions ($\textrm{rl-}w\colon 0.1 \to 1.0$) & \textbf{Stopped at step $978$ on its own registered data, and it splits cleanly into one thing bought and one thing killed.} \textbf{Bought:} the content claim, replicated at four rungs---own-minus-shuffle $+0.258 \pm 0.052$ at step $760$ against an untrained $-0.054 \pm 0.053$, clearing the bar registered before launch ($\ge +0.10$) at every rung. The thoughts carry order-borne value. \textbf{Killed:} ``train it longer and the inference win follows''. The held-out deployment drop is flat at all four rungs ($-0.443$, $-0.421$, $-0.477$, $-0.460$); paired on the same $48$ sequences, $190 \to 760$ moves $-0.017 \pm 0.052$---a third of a standard error over $570$ steps---and the training-side column agrees. The measured cause is selection, not content: the model cannot tell which of its own thoughts help (kept rate $2.9\%$ against $5.0\%$ for the control, deployment help-share pinned at $0.35$). Base gap $+0.005$. \\
\addlinespace
92 & Oct 5 & run 91 plus \textbf{abstention}: a space at thought position $0$ is a priced decision not to speak & \textbf{Stopped at step ${\approx}300$ on a mispricing in its own reward}, measured in the reward's own units by the frozen reader that computes it: the ten-space filler scored for an abstaining rollout costs $-0.792 \pm 0.063$, while the average real thought at the same anchors is worth $-0.444$. Silence was therefore priced $0.348$ nats \emph{below} a typical thought ($-0.43\sigma$ at the measured reward spread), while at deployment the identical choice costs exactly $0.000$. A correct learner shuts the gate, and run 92 did: the policy's own abstention rate fell $0.053 \to 0.032$ over $196$ steps. Two things it bought. The gate does open eight-fold at deployment ($4.2\%$ of positions against $0.5\%$ for run 91) but carries \emph{the wrong sign}---the positions it declined were better than the ones it spoke at ($-0.274 \pm 0.134$ against $-0.488 \pm 0.059$), so skipping them was worth $+0.011$ nats. And, measured here for the first time, \textbf{a perfect gate---speak only where the thought helps---turns the deployment drop from $-0.741$ into $+0.141 \pm 0.017$, $+1.12$ nats per sequence}: an upper bound computed with the label rather than an achievement, but the first positive deployed number this programme has produced in any arm. Selective silence is \emph{sufficient} to cross zero. \\
\addlinespace
93 & Oct 5 & run 92 with silence priced at exactly $0$ nats, which is what deployment pays for it (one number changed) & \textbf{Stopped at step ${\approx}990$ on the rung-4 read}, under two clauses registered independently and before the data, both of which fire. It produced \textbf{the programme's first correctly-signed, statistically real gate selectivity}: it speaks at positions whose thoughts read ${\approx}0.30$--$0.37$ nats better than the ones it declines, beating a rate-matched random gate at all four rungs ($z = +2.63$, $+2.86$, $+2.67$, $+3.26$) and the frozen initialisation by $z = +2.49$. \textbf{And it still never beat free silence}, which costs exactly $0.000$: the gated drop reads $-0.112$, $-0.225$, $-0.225$, $-0.258$ at rungs $1$--$4$, $6.8$ s.e.\ the wrong side of zero at the last one and moving away. On a fixed rung-1 yardstick the captured selectivity is $18.5\%$, $13.8\%$, $11.4\%$, $12.9\%$---flat to falling, against the ${\approx}55\%$ that would cross zero---because the gate speaks at $61.7\%$ of positions against a $31.2\%$ help rate, i.e.\ it is twice too loud. Base gap $+0.005$. \\
\midrule
94 & Oct 5--6 & run 93 with the KL-to-initialisation term \textbf{not charged on the gate decision}, so the one choice the programme exists to let the model make is not penalised for departing from the initialisation (one term's domain changed) & \textbf{Stopped at step $3{,}553$ by the owner, in favour of run 95; eighteen rungs, the longest gate run in the project, and it settles the gate route.} Selectivity stays real and correctly signed throughout (own-minus-shuffle $+0.08$ to $+0.32$, $z = 2$--$4$) and the gate cost is \emph{flat}: pooled $-0.17$ nats, with the per-rung deployment drop oscillating in $[-0.45, -0.12]$ across $3{,}420$ steps and no trend. \textbf{What removing the KL penalty bought was volume, not judgement}---the gate went from $71\%$ silent to $41\%$ silent against a help rate that stayed at $30$--$34\%$, so the extra speech was spent on positions the thought does not help. Oracle-keep and control-keep rates are interleaved at every one of the eighteen rungs ($0.021$--$0.083$ both), i.e.\ the model still cannot identify its own useful thoughts even when it is free to decline. Base gap $+0.008$. \\
\midrule
95 & Oct 6--7 & \textbf{best-of-$128$}: $128$ rollouts per spot, the reader trained on the best of them, plus gate heads ($6$ spots, $L = 6$, $K = 4$) & \textbf{Stopped at step $1{,}287$ by the owner; six rungs, and it separates selection from the policy as sharply as the project has managed.} \textbf{Selection works}: the best of $128$ rises $+1.1 \to +3.0$ nats on the scored span and $95\%$ of spots have a helpful best, and the held-out best-of-$6$ ceiling doubles, $+2.6 \to +6.0$ nats per sequence. \textbf{None of it transfers to one sample}: the held-out single thought reads $-2.66$ at the initialisation, $-1.89$ at rung $1$ and $-2.45$ at rung $6$---up once, then back down, ending where it started. So training the reader on a selected tail improves the tail and leaves the \emph{average} thought, which is what deployment draws, drifting worse. The gate head stays at chance and no label-free gate beats silence, which is run 92's $+1.12$-nat oracle bound restated from the other side: the value is in the selection, and the selector is the thing we cannot learn. Base gap $+0.003$. \\
\midrule
96 & Oct 7 & \textbf{a $50$--$50$ hint mixture}: half the micro-batches carry a \emph{gold paraphrase hint}---a true, non-verbatim summary of the continuation---in the thought slot, training reader \emph{and} writer, the other half the model's own $6$-token thoughts (RL ${+}$ reader), $K = 16$ & \textbf{Stopped at step $2{,}582$ on the registered base-damage trigger} (clean held-out mix $2.7991$ against the vanilla rung's $2.7679$ at step $2{,}470$, bar $+0.03$). Thirteen rungs. The design's premise is that a reader which has been shown what a \emph{useful} thought looks like will pay for one; the base cost of showing it is the whole of what we can report, and it is monotone---the gap to the matched vanilla rung runs $+0.004$, $+0.008$, $+0.009$, $+0.013$, $+0.015$, $+0.018$, $+0.019$, $+0.022$, $+0.024$, $+0.026$, $+0.026$, $+0.029$, $+0.031$, never once narrowing. \textbf{Later probing of this run's own checkpoints shows that the hint mixture compresses the reader's entire response to bracket content toward zero, and that it does so within this run's first $190$ steps}: a verbatim copy of the continuation is worth $+12.75$ nats at the untrained initialisation and $+5.54$ at step $190$, while a vague paraphrase goes from $-4.53$ to $-0.53$, both compressed toward zero; the remaining $1{,}900$ steps move neither. Run 94, whose design never shows the reader a hint, is \emph{indistinguishable from the untrained model on all four of these numbers after $3{,}420$ steps}, so the compression is a property of hint training and not of bracket training in general---and it is most naturally read as the reader becoming calibrated to a distribution it is now trained on, rather than as a capability lost. \\
\midrule
97 & Oct 7--8 & run 96 with the reader's weight on the model's own thoughts cut $0.5 \to 0.1$, to slow the base drift that stopped run 96 without changing the hint arm (one weight changed) & \textbf{Stopped at step $4{,}191$ by the owner on the hint arm being flat at zero.} The weight cut did what it was for---the base drift fell from $+0.0025$ to ${\approx}+0.001$ per rung and the run bought $1{,}700$ more steps than run 96 did---\textbf{and the thing it bought steps for did not move}: the held-out gold-hint $16$-token gain over five rungs reads $-0.080$, $+0.023$, $-0.004$, $+0.098$, $-0.079$, mean $-0.008$ against a standard error of $0.13$ and a registered bar of $+0.10$. The model's own thoughts cost $-0.19$ nats each at every rung, unchanged. Base gap $+0.039$ at the stop. \textbf{A correct, fluent, non-verbatim summary of what comes next, inserted in the thought slot of a reader trained for $1{,}700$ steps to expect exactly that, is worth zero nats on held-out text.} \\
\midrule
98 & Oct 8 & run 97 plus \textbf{hint-referenced reinforcement}: the model's own thought is rewarded against the gold hint at the same spot, so the RL signal no longer depends on the reader finding the thought useful (one term added), resumed from run 97's step $3{,}990$ & \textbf{Stopped at step $5{,}392$ on the registered base-damage trigger} ($2.8160$ against vanilla's $2.7625$ at step $5{,}320$, bar $+0.05$); eight rungs, and it is the programme's terminal run. The new term trains: on the training side the gold hint's gain rises $+2.72 \to +6.03$ nats per anchor over the scored $16$-token span, and $12.5\%$ of the writer's own samples beat that hint at the same spot ($10\%$ at the final step). That rate counts \emph{samples}, not spots, and the two differ: on the $56$ dumped spots the \emph{best} of the six samples beats the hint at $23\% \pm 6$, while averaging $+3.63$ nats against the hint's $+5.17$ at the same anchors---the $23\%$ is an oracle over six attempts, and the deployable number is the $12.5\%$. \textbf{Nothing deployed moves.} The model's own thoughts cost $-0.186$, $-0.212$, $-0.237$, $-0.242$, $-0.208$, $-0.225$, $-0.217$ nats each across seven rungs---flat at ${\approx}{-}0.22$, not improving---and the held-out gold-hint gain, far from rising, \emph{decays} across the two runs: $+0.098$ (step $3{,}230$), $-0.079$, $-0.23$, $-0.29$, $\ldots$, $-0.666$ (step $4{,}750$), while the hint pool is memorised harder on the training side. The gate speaks at $9\%$ and costs $-0.05$ nats per sequence. And the reward's \emph{sign} explains why: on the hint anchors---the population the advantage is computed over---the gold hint helps at $94.6\%$ of the $56$ dumped spots ($+5.17$ nats over the scored span) and the writer's own thoughts help at $82.1\%$ ($+2.82$), but only $13.4\%$ of its samples beat the hint, so at least $86.6\%$ receive a \emph{negative} advantage. At $62.5\%$ of spots ($35$ of $56$) the own thoughts help on average and not one of the six beats the hint, so every one of them is pushed down; where that happens the hint wins by $+2.35$ nats. \textbf{A reward defined as a margin against a better teacher has no way to express ``helpful, but worse than gold'', and so it suppresses most of what the writer gets right.} (These are pool-block anchors, selected because a gold paraphrase exists, and span totals over $K{=}16$; they are not the deployment anchors of Section~\ref{sec:negative}, where an own thought costs $-0.217$ nats each. The two populations must not be pooled.) \textbf{Rewarding a thought for agreeing with a correct thought teaches the writer to produce one and does not make the reader pay for it}, which is the hypothesis of the whole hint family falsified in its strongest available form. \\
\end{longtable}
}

\paragraph{The pattern.} Every failure is a shortcut: the objective offered a cheaper way to earn reward than thinking, and the optimization found it---positional invisibility aside, the shortcuts were \emph{copy the future} (run 7), \emph{repeat one safe thought} (run 8), \emph{read the leaked unigrams} (run 8), \emph{be too weird to mislead} (run 10a), \emph{score against a blind referee} (run 10b: anchors with no context, plus selection noise from keeping the best of $m$ rollouts), \emph{teach the judge your handwriting} (run 10b$'$ and again run 11: the reader co-adapts to the writer's register and manufactures value for its shape---the exploit survived a margin-based shuffle control and was closed only by an absolute register floor), and finally---run 12---\emph{no shortcut and no signal at all}: with every channel closed, the honest pass rate of a randomly-initialized thought policy against a mature reader is statistically zero, which indicts the bootstrap, not the gates. The design principles that emerged: for every baseline or gate, ask which \emph{direction} of degeneracy it leaves open, and pair it with its mirror (the floor $F$ with the absolute condition; the speculation margin with the silence bar); every threshold must be calibrated against the measurement noise of the quantity it gates, or the noise itself becomes the winning strategy; and the decisive controls are \emph{minimal pairs}---the shuffle differs from the thought only in order, the speculation rollout only in exploration---because a control that differs in many ways at once leaves room for value that is about none of them. Run 13$'$ supplied the family's terminal member: with every shortcut above closed and the reward finally honest, the cheapest remaining behavior is \emph{not thinking at all}---the gate priced to silence, lengths to the floor, the mode to one two-token stub. A shortcut-free objective does not conjure a capability; it reveals whether one is there, and at this maturity the policy's samples hold nothing the reward can pay for.

\paragraph{Where the project stands, and why nothing is running.} The question the programme opened with---do the policy's own thoughts carry order-borne value---has been answered negatively nine times, and the instruments that answer it have changed twice. Runs 15--18 measured the invariant under designs that let the model decline to think; phase A (run 21) and phase B (runs 22--24) fixed the learning rate, established that the supervised share is load-bearing at $\ge 0.10$, and left the writer plateaued at $-2.2$ nats. Run 25 then produced the project's largest held-out move, $-2.45 \to -1.17$ nats, and run 26 showed what it was: a \emph{pause} of content-free filler tokens, on the same weights, scores $-0.74$ where the written thought scores $-1.17$ (Section~\ref{sec:run26}). Runs 27 and 28 reproduced that ordering on two further independently trained checkpoints under two further reward shapes. The success bar was rewritten accordingly, and it is the bar we now hold ourselves to: \textbf{a thought must beat content-free filler of the same length on the same weights}, because everything below that line is bought compute, not thinking.

Against that bar the probes of Section~\ref{sec:probes2609} located the defect precisely. The reader is not the bottleneck and is not order-blind: shuffling a true $12$-token hint destroys $10.5$ of its $10.7$ nats, so $\mathrm{ob}_0 \approx 0$ is a statement about our writers, not about the reader. The precision ladder puts break-even at roughly $4$ of $12$ positionally-correct tokens, falling to $2$ of $12$ when the uncertain positions are filled with pause rather than context noise (Table~\ref{tab:abst})---a far lower bar than eight runs of failure had suggested. The writer's free-running thought agrees with the true continuation on $4\%$ of tokens (run 25) and, once the supervised target was moved to the immediate continuation, $11\%$ (run 28, Section~\ref{sec:run28}); the \emph{same model, teacher-forced}, is right $44\%$ of the time. \textbf{That gap between free-running and teacher-forced was the whole remaining problem, and Section~\ref{sec:tfslot} has now attributed it.} Teacher-forced from inside the thought slot, the writer averages $0.347$ over positions $1$--$11$, against a break-even of $0.167$ and a free-running $0.019$: the capability is present and above the bar, and the decoding regime alone destroys it. A follow-up on run 29 (Table~\ref{tab:tfslot29}) sharpens this: the position-$0$ collapse was an artefact of run 25's supervised target and vanishes once the writer is supervised on the immediate continuation ($0.047 \to 0.297$), while free-running agreement over positions $1$--$11$ improves $3.4\times$ at an unchanged teacher-forced ceiling and an unchanged \textsc{ntp}---still, at $1.02$ correctly-positioned tokens against the $\approx 2$ required, about half a bar short. This reorders what comes next. Scheduled sampling, which trains the regime we actually sample in rather than the teacher-forced one every run so far has trained, becomes the experiment to run; abstention and phase C stay in reserve, the first because it buys $0.03$--$0.16$ of precision headroom where the free-running deficit is $0.33$, the second because it costs four times as much per step and should not be spent before an $L{=}16$ thought is net positive.

Runs 29 and 30 have since closed the two obvious readings of that gap. Run 29 removed a five-way offset lottery so that the writer is supervised on exactly the tokens the reward scores; it produced the best free-running writer the project has measured and was stopped at step ${\approx}780$ by an $\mathrm{ob}_0$ gate that the abstention ladder has since shown cannot fire at the accuracies it was reaching---a rule we now consider to have been wrong rather than merely strict. Run 30 then applied the textbook remedy for exposure bias, scheduled sampling, and \textbf{falsified it}: it finished below its own matched control on free-running agreement and collapsed the teacher-forced ceiling it was built to exploit from $0.385$ to $0.274$ in three hundred steps (Section~\ref{sec:run30}). Feeding the model its own prefix while keeping the true token as the target asks it to learn a mapping that is not a function, and the residue is noise on shared weights. Run 31 then ran that unglamorous experiment---run 30 with scheduled sampling switched off, which is run 29
plus the aligned register---and it \textbf{failed its first gate in the most informative way available}: at
matched register and matched anchor count its own free-running agreement reads $0.0716$ at step $300$ and
$0.0722$ at step $600$, so three hundred steps moved it by $0.0006$ and run 29's apparent slope was a climb
to the same level rather than a rate of learning (Section~\ref{sec:run31}). The structural alternative---a
non-autoregressive slot, with no prefix for errors to propagate through---was measured before it could be run
and came out \emph{below} free-running autoregression on both relevant checkpoints ($0.020$ and $0.018$ over
positions $1$--$11$, against $0.071$ and $0.069$); we read that as a lower bound on an untrained regime
rather than a refutation, but it is not the escape. Exposure bias, decoding order and optimization time have
now each had a run or a probe of their own, and the free-running writer sits in the same place after all of
them. \textbf{We therefore stop trying to raise the writer's accuracy and start reducing what its errors
cost.} Run 32 supervised the writer to speak only where the next token is forecastable and to emit
content-free filler everywhere else---the interleaved-pause format that the abstention ladder measured as
readable, and which drops break-even from roughly four correctly-placed tokens of twelve to two
(Section~\ref{sec:run32}). It stopped the writer from harming without making it help: own-minus-filler
settled at $-0.130 \pm 0.033$ and stayed there. Run 33 attacked the term that decomposition says is five
to six times larger---the price of splicing the block in at all---and \textbf{it was stopped at step $899$
when the mechanism reversed}: the block's price fell to $-0.551$ by step $400$ and then rose again to
$-0.664$, a quarter of the available headroom rather than the two-thirds its gate required
(Section~\ref{sec:run33}). Reading its value gate exposed a defect that retires a number we had reported:
on an abstaining lineage the model's greedy thought \emph{is} the filler control---space tokens and a
closing marker---so own-minus-filler on that instrument measures length, and at matched length run 32 has
no measurable content effect at all. \textbf{Run 34 asked the question that exposes---is a token the model chooses to write worth more, to the
same model reading it, than leaving the position empty?---and answered it no} (Section~\ref{sec:run34}).
Run 32 reached its best value by ceasing to speak---filler at $88\%$ of positions---so run 34 removed the
loss term that made silence a supervised action while keeping the format, and its gates required the writer
to speak before any value reading was accepted. It spoke: the filler rate fell to $0.043$, its opener and
its teacher-forced ceiling recovered, and its free-running token agreement rose above run 32's. Its value
fell with it, to $-0.520 \pm 0.067$ at a four-token prefix, below even the unabstaining control it started
from. Across the three checkpoints own-minus-filler is a function of the rate at which the writer emits a
non-blank token and not of how often that token is right, which retires run 32's apparent value as a
selection effect of silence. That is the form in which we register the negative result: at this scale a
token the model chooses to write is worth less to it than a blank, and the cost scales with how often it
chooses to write one. The benchmarks of Section~\ref{sec:bench} have now been run on that final checkpoint, and they close the result rather than qualify it: the five-task discrimination gain that run 21 showed is gone ($+0.12$ points, $p = 0.91$), a thought at inference costs $1.4$ macro points against the same weights in clean mode ($p = 4\times10^{-4}$), and the thought being scored is $258$ distinct strings over $4{,}000$ examples---a register, not a forecast. One line in them is encouraging and it is the same one: making the writer speak is downstream-free. Paired against run 34's own initialisation the whole benchmark suite moves by macro $-0.4$ ($p = 0.47$ on the pre-registered pool), so the capability the objective has cost was spent earlier, on recalibrating the reader, and not on the speech. What is missing is not permission to speak; it is anything worth saying.

The paragraph that follows was written when no run was live; the thirty-two runs since have not changed any of its verdicts, and Section~\ref{sec:synthetic} and Section~\ref{sec:slide} record them. Each axis the programme opened has been closed by a measurement and not by exhaustion: writer accuracy by runs 28, 30 and 31 and the parallel-slot probe, which put four different levers against the same flat floor; the decoding regime by run 30's falsification; the choice of \emph{where} to speak by run 32 for frequency and, without spending a run at all, by the count-matched ceiling probes for confidence and its opposite; the price of the block itself by run 33, whose mechanism reversed at step $400$; and speaking against abstaining by run 34, which found value tracking volume and not correctness. Four further runs have since closed the two escapes that remained. The \emph{task}: run 35 replaced forecasting with carrying, a job the writer can do at $0.30$--$0.41$ per token teacher-forced, and its probe found that the reader will not pay for the text it is missing at all---$-0.27 \pm 0.18$ against $+9.85 \pm 0.59$ for a forecast under the same mask (Section~\ref{sec:run35}). That is the more general statement of the negative, and it retires \emph{every} redesign whose thesis is an easier target. The \emph{medium}: runs 37--39 gave the writer $64$ reserved ids with no pretrained meaning and asked the pair to invent a code for them, under reinforcement (37), under a supervised hash of the upcoming window (38), and---as a bounded control with the reward switched off and the target reduced to the hash of a single token already in context (39)---under conditions as favourable to the channel as the design allows. The alphabet never separated in any of the three, and run 39 recovered $0.141$ of the $0.148$ nats per symbol available from a context-free marginal and none of the $4.011$ available from its own context (Section~\ref{sec:code}). Those runs are also, by run 35's table, the clearest case of the discipline this paper argues for failing us: a private code is not forecast-shaped text, so the measurement that closed them was already on file before the first of them was launched. Phase C is gated on an $L{=}16$ thought being net positive on held-out text, which has not happened
once in seventy runs. Three things have been added to the record since. The \emph{corpus}: runs 40--59
moved to a synthetic task where a correct note is provably worth many nats to the same reader, and the
reader learned to use one at exact-match $+0.510$ ($p = 5\times10^{-21}$) while the writer stayed at
exactly zero (Section~\ref{sec:synthetic}). The \emph{curriculum}: runs 60--69 slid the scored span from
compression to forecast, and its decomposition found the writer's reinforcement term to be
\emph{protective}---removing it costs $-1.334 \pm 0.417$---while doubling it buys nothing measurable,
$+0.103 \pm 0.306$ over seven rungs (Section~\ref{sec:slide}). The \emph{instrument}: those same runs
showed the reader degrading the baselines it is used to score against, which is why runs 70--81 freeze the reader and gate against the untrained model's own readings (Section~\ref{sec:frozen}); run 81, hindsight distilled into foresight under a contrastive reward, is the run live as we write this. We
would still rather record an idle node than improvise another run onto an axis a probe has already
closed, which is the failure mode the pre-registered gates in this paper exist to prevent.

\end{document}
